ulaka makkaḷ tokai vivaraṅkaḷ palvēṟu nūlkaḷiliruntu eṭukkappaṭṭavai. ki.mu.500, ki.pi.1, ki.pi. 1600 ākiya āṇṭukaḷil intiya makkaḷ tokai vivaraṅkaḷ rōmilā tāpar eḻutiya paṇṭaiya intiyāviṉ peṅkuyiṉ varalāṟu (2002) puttakattiṟ kaṇṭavai.

2. mekarkār eṉṉum iṭattil ceyta akaḻvāykaḷukkup piṉ, cintu nākarikattiṉ muntaiya toṭakka nilaikkāṉa cāṉṟukaḷ ki.mu.7000 liruntu toṭarcciyākak kiṭaikkiṉṟaṉa. cintu nākarikam intiyāviṟkuḷḷēyē tōṉṟi iṭaiyīṭiṉṟip paṭippaṭiyāka mutircci peṟṟu vēta kālattukku (ki.mu.1500-) vekumuṉṉar iruntē nilaipeṟṟu iruntatu.

3. āṭu māṭu vaḷarttal, tavacaṅkaḷ payiriṭutal, parutti payiriṭutalpōṉṟa tuṟaikaḷil cintu nākarikattiṉ tolvaravukkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷai mekarkār āyvu veḷikkoṇarntuḷḷatu vēḷāṇmaiyē nāṭṭuppuṟa makkaḷiṉ perun toḻilāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum. āṭu māṭu mēyttuk koṇṭu avvap poḻutu kuṭipeyarntu vāḻntu vantavarkaḷum (pastoral nomads and cattle keeping tribes) iruntiruppar. cintup pakutiyil ki.mu.3000il irunta taṭpaveppa nilai iṉṟu uḷḷatu pōltāṉ iruntirukkum eṉpar pocel (2002:15 the climate of this region was not markedly different in the third millennium B.C. from the one we have today”. 

4. kalipaṅkaṉil 5000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar uḻutu pātti kaṭṭiya amaippu (toṭakkakālac cintu nākarikattaic cārntatu) kiṭaittuḷḷatu kuṟippiṭattakkatu. kōtumaiyum pārliyum mukkiyap payirkaḷākavum, uṇavup poruḷkaḷākavum iruntaṉa. cōḷam, kampu, kēḻvarakum payirāyiṉa. lōtalil nel umi akaḻvāyvil kaṇṭu piṭikkappaṭṭatu. ulakilēyē mutalil paruttit tuṇi uṟpatti ceyta nāṭu intiyāvē. mekarkāril parutti ki.mu.5000 kku muṉṉarē payaṉpaṭuttappaṭṭuḷḷatu. (cumēriyāvil piṉṉar paruttit tuṇikku ‘cintu’ eṉṟē peyar vaḻaṅkiyatu) kirāmap puṟaṅkaḷil vēḷāṇmai ceḻippāka iruntamaiyiṉāltāṉ mokañcotarō, hārappā mutaliya peru nakaraṅkaḷukkut tēvaiyāṉa tavacaṅkaḷ taṭaiyiṉṟik kiṭaittaṉa. mokañcotarō, hārappā nakaraṅkaḷil kaṇṭupiṭitta cila kaṭṭaṭa eccaṅkaḷ muṉṉar tāṉiyak kaḷañciyaṅkaḷ eṉak kūṟap paṭṭu vantaṉa. āṉāl aṇmaikkālak karuttu avai pala poruḷkaḷaiyum vaikkum kiṭaṅkukaḷ (warehouses) tām, tāṉiyak kaḷañciyaṅkaḷ alla eṉpatākum (pocel: 2002. pak 247)

5. kutirai tavira iṉṟu cintup pakutiyil uḷḷa aṉaittu vilaṅkukaḷum aṉṟum iruntaṉa. iṉṟu appakutiyil illāta yāṉaikaḷum kāṇṭāmirukaṅkaḷumkūṭa aṉṟu cintup pakutiyil iruntaṉa.

6. (i) kaṉimap poruḷkaḷil irumpu tavira ēṉaiya paṇṭai māḻaikaḷiṉāl āṉa poruṭkaḷ (veṇkala aram, kōṭari, ampu muṉaikaḷ; īṭṭimuṉaikaḷ, ūcikaḷ, ēṉaṅkaḷ) kiṭaittuḷḷaṉa. irumpum urukkum teṉṉintiyāvilum kiḻakku intiyāvilum tāṉ ulakilēyē mutaṉ mutalil irumput tātuviliruntu uṟpatti ceyyappaṭṭavaiyākat tōṉṟukiṉṟaṉa. eṉiṉum cintuveḷi nākarika muṭivu kālamākiya ki.mu.1800 aḷavil aṅku irumpu kuṟippiṭattakka aḷavukkup paravavillai. ataṟkup piṉṉar intiyāviṟkuḷ nuḻainta āriyarkaḷum irumpaik koṇṭu vantavarkaḷ allar. ‘āriyarkaḷ irumpuk karuvikaḷuṭaṉ vantaṉar. eṉavē eḷitil veṉṟaṉar’. eṉpatu kaṟpaṉai eṉṟu kārṭaṉ kūṟukiṟār. (D.H. Gordon: The Prehistoric background of Indian culture; 1960) rik vētam ‘ayam’ eṉak kūṟuvatu potuvāka cempu pōṉṟa māḻaiyaiyē; irumpai alla. tamiḻil poṉ eṉpatu māḻaikkup potup peyarāka vaḻaṅkiyataṉṟō. [irum (karuppu) + poṉ - irumpoṉ - irumpu] ceppuk karuvikaḷōṭu kaṟ karuvikaḷaiyum toṭarntu payaṉpaṭuttiṉar cintu veḷiyiṉar.

(ii) akaḻvāyvil kiṭaitta cāṉṟukaḷiliruntu cempu, veṇkalap poruḷ kammiyar, kaṟkaruvikaḷil ceyta kaṭṭaṭa vēlaikkārar, muttirai ceyyunar pōṉṟa palavakait toḻil viṟpaṉṉarkaḷ aṉṟu iruntamai teḷivākiṟatu.

(iii) ti. cattiyamūrtti (2009) kaṭṭurai Indus to Tamaraparni – il ceppukkalavai (Copper alloy) payaṉpāṭṭil mokañcotarōvukkum āticcanallūrukkum irunta oru oppumaiyaic cuṭṭukiṟār. cempil 1% ārceṉik - ai vēṇṭumeṉṟē kalantu ak kalavaiyiṉ vaṉmaiyai (hardness) 124 VHN liruntu 177VHN atikarittuḷḷaṉar. intiyāvil toṉmaic ceppukkalavai ēṉaṅkaḷil mokeñcotarōvilum, āticcanallūrilum kiṭaittavaṟṟil maṭṭumē ārceṉik 4% m, ataṟku mēlum uḷḷatu. āticca nallūr, mokañcotarō nākarikaṅkaḷiṉ orumaippāṭṭukkāṉa cāṉṟukaḷil oṉṟāka itaiyum karutalāmeṉpār avar.

7. (i) cintu nākarikap perunakara makkaḷ ceḻittu vāḻntataṟku vāṇikamē aṭippaṭai. uḷnāṭṭu vāṇikattil intiyāviṉ pala pakutikaḷiliruntum koṇarnta vilaimatippuḷḷap poruṭkaḷ paṅkēṟṟaṉa. amecāṉ eṉṉum paccaikkal nīlakirip pakutiyiliruntum, caṅku, muttu mutaliyavai pāṇṭiya nāṭṭiṉiṉṟum koṇṭu cellappaṭṭirukkalām. kaṭal vaḻiyākavum, āṟukaḷ vaḻiyākavum poruḷkaḷ paṭakukaḷil aṉuppappaṭṭatuṭaṉ, uḷnāṭṭil erutukaḷaiyum kaḻutaikaḷaiyum vaṇikac cāttu vilaṅkukaḷākap payaṉpaṭuttiyiruppar. malaip pakutikaḷiliruntu cakki mukkik kal (flint) rājastāṉ pōṉṟa pakutikaḷiliruntu cemputtātu, mahārāṣṭiratti liruntu cevvantikkal, caurāṣṭirattiliruntu akēṭ ((Agate) kaṟkaḷ; karunāṭakattiliruntu taṅkam pōṉṟavai uḷnāṭṭu vāṇikap poruḷkaḷil cilavākum.

(ii) cintu nākarikap pakutikaḷililēyē ciṟanta pācimaṇikaḷ uṟpatti ceyyappaṭṭaṉa. 1200 ṭikiri celciyas varaic cūṭēṟṟi maṇikaḷait tayārikkum ulaikaḷ kalipaṅkaṉil kiṭaittuḷḷaṉa.

8. ayalnāṭukaḷōṭu, kuṟippāka mecapotāmiyāvuṭaṉ perumaḷavu vāṇikam naṭantuḷḷatu. mecapotāmiyāvil cumār 24 hārappā muttiraikaḷ kiṭaittuḷḷaṉa. mecapotāmiya ciliṇṭar muttiraikaḷ cila mokeñcotarōvil kiṭaittuḷḷaṉa. mecapo tāmiya cācaṉaṅkaḷil melukā (Meluhha) eṉṉum nāṭṭiliruntu karuṅkāli maram, rattiṉak kaṟkaḷ, tantam pōṉṟavai iṟakkumati ceyyappaṭṭatākak kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. melukā eṉpatu cintu veḷiyait tāṉ kuṟittatu eṉpatai aṉaivarum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. (parpōlā, 1975; rōmilā tāpar 1975, ālciṉ 1988, leviṭ 2009) mecapotāmiyā vukku paruttit tuṇi perumaḷavukku ēṟṟumatiyāki yirukkavēṇṭum. vāṇikattil (veḷināṭu maṟṟum uḷnāṭu) cippaṅkaḷaik kaṭṭi muttiraikaḷ iṭṭaṉar. āyirakkaṇakkāṉa muttiraikaḷ eḻuttukaḷuṭaṉ kiṭaittuḷḷaṉa. ivaipaṟṟi piṉ iyalil kāṇka. vāṇikat tiṟkāka rattiṉak kaṟkaḷ kiṭṭum (vaṭakiḻakku āpkāṉistāṉil uḷḷa) ṣōrttukay (Shortugai) eṉṉum iṭattil cintu veḷi nākarika makkaḷ (vaṇikar?) kuṭiyiruppu iruntatu akaḻvāyvil kaṇṭu piṭikkappaṭṭuḷḷatu. cintuveḷi nākarika eṭai aḷavukaḷ tamiḻariṭaiyē vaḻaṅkiyavaiyē. itai piṉvarum iyalil kāṇka.

9. cintu nākarikattukkum elām, cumēriya nākarikaṅkaḷukkum iṭaiyil mika neruṅkiya toṭarpu ki.mu.4000 ttiliruntē iruntu vantatu eṉpatai mārṣal mutal pocel varai aṉaivarum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar [eṉiṉum tirāviṭa moḻipēcunar ēṟṟattil Dravidian Ascent  teṟkiliruntu ceṉṟa tirāviṭarkaḷ tām cintuppakutiyil ciṟanta nākarikattait tōṟṟuvittup piṉṉar elām cumēriya nākarikaṅkaḷaiyum uruvākkiṉar eṉṉum hīrās pōṉṟavar kaḷuṭaiya karuttu iṉṟaiya varaiyil ittuṟaiyil valla paṉṉāṭ ṭaṟiñar perumpālōr ēṟpiṉaip peṟavillai] āyiṉum am mūṉṟu nākarikaṅ kaḷukkum iṭaiyilāṉa vāṇikat toṭarpu perum pālum mēṭṭukkuṭiyiṉar payaṉpaṭuttum āṭamparap poruḷkaḷ (rattiṉak kaṟkaḷ, kalaipporuḷkaḷ, māḻaip poruṭkaḷ, paruttit tuṇi mutaliyavai) cārntatākavē iruntatu. ki.mu.2000 iliruntu orupāl im mūṉṟu nākarikaṅkaḷukkum, maṟupāl avaṟṟukku vaṭakkil irunta pākṭiriyāṉā mārjiyāṉā tolliyal paṇpāṭṭu (pā.mā.to.-BMAC) nākarikattukkum vāṇikattoṭarpu iruntirukkum eṉṟum, mokañcotarōviṉ iṟuti nūṟu, irunūṟu āṇṭukaḷil pā.mā.to. paṇpāṭṭu makkaḷ cintu nākarikap perumparappiṟ kuḷḷum varattoṭaṅkiyirukkalām eṉṟum pocel (2002:235) karutukiṟār.

10. cintu muttiraikaḷil oṉṟu vilaṅkukaḷ cūḻa irukkum civaṉaik (pacupatiyaik) kāṭṭuvatākak koḷḷappaṭukiṟatu. vaṭa airōppāvil cermaṉikku vaṭakkē ṭeṉmārk nāṭṭil kaṇtastirap eṉṉum iṭattil 28-5-1891 il kaṇṭeṭukkappaṭṭa 21/4aṭi kuṟukkaḷavuḷḷa veḷḷik kopparaiyiṉ mēlē poṟikkappaṭṭuḷḷa uruvaṅkaḷil anta pacupati pōṉṟa uruvamum uḷḷatu. akkopparai ki.mu.200il ceyyappaṭṭatu. cintu veḷik kāla poṉ - veḷḷik kalaiñarkaḷ paiyappaiya mēlai nāṭukaḷilum paraviyiruntataṉ kāraṇamākat tāṉ avvuruvam ap pāttirattil poṟikkappaṭa nērntatu eṉpatai viḷakkum ‘kaṇtastirap kopparai’ eṉṉum kaṭṭuraiyaicayaṉṭipik amerikkaṉ 1992 mārc itaḻil timōtti ṭēlar eḻutiyuḷḷār.

11(i) cintuveḷi makkaḷ vīṭukaḷil ammiyum kuḻaviyum, uralum payaṉpaṭuttiṉar. maṭpāṇṭak kalai ciṟappāka iruntatu. pāṉaikaḷil cittira vēlaippāṭu iruntatu. pala tuḷaikaḷ iṭṭa kaṉaṟcaṭṭi payaṉ paṭuttiṉar. vīṭukaḷil tāṉiyaṅkaḷaik koṭṭi vaikkat tāḻikaḷai (kutirkaḷai) payaṉpaṭuttiṉar. kuḻantaikaḷiṉ viḷaiyāṭṭup poruḷkaḷ (paṟavai vaṭivil ūtu kuḻalkaḷ; talai acaikkum erutukaḷ; kōlikaḷ) ērāḷamākak kiṭaittuḷḷaṉa.

(ii) kaḷimaṇṇāṟ ceyta pommai vaṇṭikaḷ cila kiṭaittuḷḷaṉa. ārakkāl illāta cakkaraṅkaḷaik koṇṭavai. itē pōṉṟa vaṇṭikaḷ iṉṟum cintu mānilattil uḷḷaṉa.

(iii) ammakkaḷ poṉ, veḷḷi, veṇpoṉ (Electrum) cempu, veṇkalam, tantam, elumpu, mākkal, irattiṉak kaṟkaḷ, cuṭṭakaḷimaṇ, caṅku mutaliyavaṟṟaik koṇṭu tattam takutikkēṟṟa aṇikaḷai vitam vitamākac ceytu aṇintu vantaṉar. kiṭaittuḷḷa aḻakiya aṇikalaṉkaḷil cila: kaḻuttu mālai, oṭṭiyāṇam, āṟu carakkai
yaṇi, arakku uḷḷīṭu koṇṭa kāppu, palavakai vaḷaiyal, kāl kāppu (taṇṭai), neṟṟic cuṭṭi, kātaṇi, mōtiram, pottāṉ, talaināṭā, koṇṭai ūci pōṉṟavai ākum.

12. intiyāviṉ taṟkālak kalai, toḻil nuṭpaviyal tuṟaikaḷ palavaṟṟilum cintu nākarikak kāla vērkaḷaik kāṇalām eṉpār ṭi.pi. akarvāl (2009, Harappan Technology and its legacy).. palatuṟaikaḷilum am māperum cintu nākarikap pakuti muḻuvatilum eytiyirunta toḻilnuṭpac cātaṉainilai paṇṭaiya ulaka nākarikam etilum kāṇātatu eṉalām. (ālciṉ: “the technical level achieved over so great an area as is demonstrated in the Harappan civilisation is probably unique in the ancient world”). kaṟpaṉaivaḷamikka kalaip paṭaippukkaḷai mikutiyākak kāṇa iyalavillai eṉpatai ālciṉ cuṭṭik kāṭṭiviṭṭu “oru vēḷai avarkaḷ taṅkaḷ kaṟpaṉaik kalait tiṟaṉaik kāṭṭum cātaṉaikaḷait tuṇikaḷ āṭaikaḷ pōṉṟa tuṟaikaḷil kāṭṭiyirukkalām. (avai namakku iṉṟu kiṭṭavillai)” eṉṟu karutukiṟār.

kalai vaḷarcci

13. hārappāvil kiṭaitta 10 ce.mī. uyarac cevvantikkal Red Jasper āṭavaṉ cilai (kaḻuttiliruntu toṭaivarai) ki.mu. 2200 - 1900 kālattatu, (talai, kaikaḷai iṇaippataṟkāṉa tuḷaikaḷum uḷḷaṉa.)

14. mokañcotarō DK 1909 pakutiyil kiṭṭiya cuṇṇāmpuk kallil cetukkiya pūcāri - aracaṉ Priest - King’ 18 ce.mī. paṭivamum kuṟippiṭattakkatu, atil cittirap pū vēlai ceyta pōrvai, oḻuṅkāṉa tāṭi, talaiyil muṭiyaic cīvip paṭṭai kaṭṭiyuḷḷatu ākiyavaṟṟaik kāṇkiṟōm. atu pūcāriyumalla, aracaṉumalla, kaṟpaṉai vaṭivamē eṉpar pocel.

15. veṇkalattāṟ ceyyappaṭṭa (11 ce.mī. uyara) naṭaṉamātiṉ cilaiyum cintu veḷi naṭaṉak kalaikkuc cāṉṟākum. harappāvil kiṭṭiya 10 ce.mī uyara cāmpal niṟakkaṟ cilai oṉṟu uṇarcciyuṭaṉ naṭaṉam ceyya toṭaṅkuvatu pōla iṭatu kālait tūkki valakkāl mītu niṉṟu naṭaṉam āṭa niṟpatu pōlat) tōṉṟukiṉṟatu. piṟ kālattil tamiḻ nāṭṭil ki.pi.1000 ai oṭṭi āṭavallāṉukku (naṭarāca ṉukku) ciṟanta paṭimaṅkaḷ amaikkappaṭṭaṉavaṉṟō avaṟṟukkāṉa tōṟṟuvāy cintu veḷiyilē ceyyappaṭṭirukkalāmō eṉpatu caṅkāliyā pōṉṟavarkaḷ karuttu.

16. intiya icaiyiṉ varalāṟu eṉṉum nūlil ō. kōsvāmi icait tuṟaiyilum āriyar nuḻainta kālattoṭakkattiliruntu āriyarukku muntiya (tamiḻ) icaikkūṟukaḷ [aṉṟu tamiḻiyac cintu nākarikattilum avai iruntirukkum] perumaḷavil cērntuḷḷatai viḷakkukiṟār. “intiya icaikku cāmavētamē piṟappiṭam eṉṟa) kaṟpaṉaik kūṟṟai muṉvaittukkoṇṭu icai aṟivukkum payiṟcikkum āriyar tamakku muṉ attuṟaiyil vallōrāka irunta āriyarallātāriṭam kaṭaṉpaṭṭiruppatai maṟakkaṭikka muṭintuḷḷatu” eṉkiṟār. [By this fiction (that Saman is the source of all Indian music) alone it was possible to forget conveniently the debt the Aryans owed to the non – Aryans for their musical knowledge and practice.” The Story of Indian Music; 1957] 

cāmavēta icaikkum, potuvāka intiya icai muḻumaikkum, tamiḻicaiyē aṭippaṭaiyāṉatu eṉpatai Tamil Culture  itaḻil 1958- 59l (VII-1 & VIII-3) eḻutiya kaṭṭuraikaḷilum ‘tamiḻicai tanta peru veḷḷam’ [1978: tamiḻaka nuṇkalaikaḷ (tamiḻp paṇpāṭṭu maṉṟam / pāri nilaiyam, nūliṉ pak. 12-66] eṉṉum kaṭṭuraiyilum ku. kōtaṇṭapāṇi piḷḷai ātāraṅkaḷuṭaṉ niṟuviyuḷḷār.

17. ki.mu.3200 - 2600 kālattiya toṭakkanilai (Early Harappan) cintu nākarikamāṉatu nūṟu āṇṭukālam (2600 - 2500) vaḷarnilaic cintu nākarikamāka urumāṟat toṭaṅkip piṉṉar ki.mu.2500 – 1900 (mottam 600 āṇṭukaḷ) vaḷarnilai cintu Mature Harappan nākarika māka āṉatu evvāṟu nikaḻntirukkalām eṉpatu paṟṟiya pocel uṉṉippukaḷaic (2002: 47-53, 55-58) curukkamākak kāṇalām. cintu nākarika makkaḷiṭam maṭṭumē viḷaṅkiya oru taṉi laṭciyak kōṭpāṭē (ideology)  vaḷarnilaic cintu nākarikattai uruvākkiyatu. cintu nākarikattil maṭṭumē kāṇum piṉvarum nāṉku taṉmaikaḷai yum uḷḷaṭakkiyatu ak kōṭpāṭu:-

18. antak kālakaṭṭattil (ki.mu.3000 - 2000) paṇṭaiya nākarika nāṭukaḷil aracu State eṉpatu eppaṭi iruntirukkum eṉpatu paṟṟi tolliya lāḷar, varalāṟṟāciriyar pōṉṟa paltuṟai aṟiñarkaḷiṭam orumitta karuttu illai. eṉiṉum avvaraciṉ taṉmaikaḷil piṉvaru vaṉavum iruntirukkum eṉṟu avarkaḷ karutukiṉṟaṉar:

periya aḷavu poruḷātāra, vaṇika naṭavaṭikkaikaḷ; taṉittaṉik kaittoḻilkaḷil viṟpaṉṉarkaḷ; niruvākap paṇiyaik kavaṉittavarkaḷ (kaṇakkar, mēṟpārvaiyāḷar uṭpaṭa) potu makkaḷiṭaiyē mēl / kīḻ pirivukaḷ (perumpālum aracar eṉa aḻaikkappaṭṭa) talaivar / talaivarkaḷ – potumakkaḷaik kaṭṭuppaṭutti oṟukkum atikārattuṭaṉ; at talaivarkaḷukku periya mukkiyattuvam; aracu cārnta camayam, kōyilkaḷ paṟṟiya taṭayaṅkaḷ

ippaṭippaṭṭa kūṟukaḷaik koṇṭa aracu cintu nākarikattil iruntataṟkāṉa taṭayaṅkaḷ illai eṉpar pocel (2002:6) “The socio - cultural form that the Indus polity took is not known”. eṉavē cintu nākarikam aṟṟai mocopotāmiya, ekiptu nākarikaṅkaḷaip pōlum, piṟṟai (ki.pi.nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil irunta vaṭa amerikka mayā, teṉamerikka peru nāṭṭu iṅkā nākarikaṅkaḷaip pōlum paṉmuka vaḷarcciyaṭaintiruntapōtilum (mēṟkaṇṭa aṭaiyāḷaṅkaḷai uṭaiya) aracu etuvum cintu nākarikattil illai. pūcāri - aracarkaḷum Priest Kings cintu nākarikattil illai. piṉ eppaṭippaṭṭa amaippu an nākarikattaip pala nūṟu āṇṭukaḷ aṅku pēṇi naṭattivantatu eṉpatai iṉṉum tolliyalāḷarkaḷ orumitta karuttuṭaṉ niṟuvavillai; ulakilēyē ittaṉmai vāynta nākarikam vēṟu etuvum eṅkum eṉṟum iruntatu “To my knowledge, there is, for example, no close parallel to it, in either the Archaeological or Ethnographic record.” eṉiṉum, cintu nākarikattai niruvakittu vanta amaippu (ēṉaiya kīḻai nāṭukaḷaip pōṉṟu) koṭuṅkōṉmai vāyntatākat tōṉṟavillai. ammakkaḷiṉ aṉaittup piriviṉarumē ōraḷavu camattuvattuṭaṉ ceḻittu vāḻntaṉar eṉak karutalām (There is no reason for us to believe that it was despotic, in the oriental fashion p.247 [no basis] to rule out the notion that the entire Indus population was generally well-off, possibly relatively egalitarian (p.175) ivaiyē pocel karuttukaḷ.)

19. ulakil vēṟu paṇṭaiya nākarikam etilum illāta oru taṉik kōṭpāṭṭaik ṉainaṭiṭaṭipala koṇṭu uruvāṉa cintu nākarika vīḻccikku antak kōṭpāṭṭiṉpaṭi ceyalpaṭṭa amaippukaḷē cumār 600 āṇṭukaḷukku mēl akkōṭpāṭṭait toṭarntu pēṇi varamuṭiyāta nilai ēṟpaṭṭatē (a flaw in internal structure)  kāraṇamākalām. evvaḷavutāṉ nallatāka iruntālum oru kōṭpāṭu nirantaramāka nilaipeṟṟu, aḻivillāmal irukka muṭiyumā? mēlē viḷakkiyatum, neṭuṅkālam muraṇpāṭu conflict  iṉṟi nilaittirunta cintuk kōṭpāṭum Indus ideology) oru kālattil muṭivukku vantu tāṉē ākavēṇṭum; (“too well adapted for its own good;” too much of a good thing) eṉavē cintu nākarikam muṭivaṭaintataṟku mēlē iyal 1 iṉ iṟutiyil ālciṉ kuṟitta kāraṇaṅkaḷuṭaṉ ikkāraṇattaiyum cērttuk koḷḷalām.

20. maikkēl vuṭ (2007: The story of India) nālāyiram muttiraikaḷ mutaliyavai kiṭaittiruppiṉum pōr, kolai, koḷḷai, rattak kaḷari, ‘veṉṟavaṉ muṉ kaitikaḷ’ pōṉṟa kāṭcikaḷ cittarikkap paṭātatai viyakkiṟār. ki.mu. nāṉkām nūṟṟāṇṭil alek cāṇṭaruṭaṉ intiyāvukku vanta kirēkkarkaḷum‘intiyarkaḷ akkiramap pōrai veṟuttaṉar; aṟaneṟi pōṟṟiṉar’ eṉa eḻutukiṉṟaṉar. vaṉ muṟaiyai veṟutta piṟṟai intiya camayaṅkaḷ camaṇam, pauttam (av viraṇṭukkum muntiyatāka ka. neṭuñceḻiyaṉ mutaliyavarkaḷ karutum ācīvakam) ākiyaṉavellām cintu nākarikak kālattilēyē karuk koṇṭavai eṉṟu pala aṟiñar karutuvataic camayam paṟṟiya piṉ varum iyalil kāṇka. ik karutukōḷ cariyeṉṟāl vaṉ muṟaiyē aṭippaṭaiyākak koṇṭa im mānta iṉattiṉ varalāṟṟil cintu nākarikam taṉippaṭṭa ciṟappuṭaiyatu eṉpar vuṭ (But if anything like that were true, it would be unique in the violent history of humanity)

naṭaṉa mātiṉ paṭivam- veṇkalam 		tāḻip putaippu

iyal 5

cintuveḷi eṭai aḷavukaḷ
(tamiḻariṉ eṭai aḷavukaḷai ottavai)

tamiḻariṭaiyē aṇmaik kālam varai naṭaimuṟaiyil irunta “ciṟṟilakka” aṭippaṭaiyil amainta eṇ muṟaiyē cintu veḷi nākarikattilum naṭaimuṟaiyil iruntatu eṉṟu kaṇṭaṟintu veḷiyiṭṭa perumai poṟiyiyal aṟiñar ceṅkam vaḷaiyāmpaṭṭu ku. vēṅkaṭācalam uṭaiyatākum. (A study of the Weights and Measures of the Indus valley civilisation; 1983 tamiḻp palkalaik kaḻakak karuttaraṅkuk kaṭṭurai) ituvarai ulakil kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭuḷḷa eṭai aḷavu muṟaikaḷilēyē toṉmaiyāṉatu itutāṉ (Berriman: Historical Metrology; 1953)

mēṟkaṇṭa piṉṉaṅkaḷukku iṭaippaṭṭa cila maṭaṅkukaḷum tamiḻ nāṭṭil iruntaṉa. cintu veḷiyil kaṇṭeṭutta eṭaikaḷellām ic ciṟṟilakka vāyppāṭṭu eṇmāṉa aṭippaṭaiyil amaintaṉavē eṉpatai vēṅkaṭācalam teḷivāka viḷakkiyuḷḷār. eṭai aḷavukaḷ paṟṟi vētaṅkaḷ, arttacāstiram pōṉṟavaṟṟuḷ araikuṟaiyākac collappaṭṭuḷḷavaiyum cintuveḷi eṭaikaḷaip (ciṟṟilakka aṭippaṭaiyila maintavai) piṉpaṟṟiyavaiyē eṉpataiyum; intiyāvil palvēṟu pakutikaḷilum (1956 l meṭrik eṭaic caṭṭam naṭaimuṟaikku varum varai) naṭaimuṟaiyilirunta eṭai aḷavukaḷ ellāmē cintuveḷi (tamiḻiya) eṭai aḷavukaḷaip piṉpaṟṟi yavaiyē eṉpataiyum avarniṟuviyuḷḷār. ki.mu.500kku muntiya tolkāppiya cūttiraṅkaḷilēyē eṭai, eṇmāṉa, aḷavīṭṭuc coṟkaḷ pala kuṟippiṭappaṭṭu avaṟṟukkāṉa puṇarcci mutaliya ilakkaṇa vitikaḷ kūṟappaṭṭuḷḷatai vēṅkaṭācalam viḷakki tamiḻariṭaiyē ikkōṭpāṭukaḷiṉ toṉmaiyaic cuṭṭukiṟār.

4. tamiḻarukkēyuriya 84cemī (33 aṅkula = iraṇṭēmukkāl aṭi) taccumuḷam cintuveḷik kālattiliruntu kaṭanta 5000 āṇṭukaḷākat toṭarntu vantuḷḷamaiyai koṭu muṭi caṇmukam kuṟittuḷḷār. (Journal of Tamil Studies, cūṉ 1980) cintu veḷit
terukkaḷil pala 33’, 11’ aṭi eṉṟa aḷavil iruntataik kaṇṭōm. 11 eṉpatu 23/4 aṭiyiṉ nāṉku maṭaṅku lōtalil kaṇṭeṭukkappaṭṭa caṅku ōṭṭil ceyta oru ciṟiya aṭikkōlil 1.32 aṅkulam 5 pakutikaḷāka (ovvoṉṟum 0.264 aṅkulam) pirikkappaṭṭuḷḷatu. taccumuḻam 33 aṅkulattai 25 ākap pirittāl ovvoru pirivum 1.32 aṅkulam varum eṉpatai nōkkuka. tañcaip peruvuṭaiyār kōvilum tiruvaṇṇāmalaiyār kōyilum iraṇṭē mukkāl aṭi taccumuḻa aḷaviṉ paṭiyē kaṭṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa.

iyal 6

camayam

1. camayam (matam) eṉpatu teyvaṅkaḷaik kuṟittu māntar nampuvataik (maṟupiṟavi, maṟuulaku, toṉmaṅkaḷ uṭpaṭa) kuṟippa tākum. annampikkaikaḷ meyppikkavō, poyppikkavō muṭiyātavai (can be neither affirmed nor falsified) kaṭavuḷ, āṉmikam cārnta inta nampikkaiyaip pōṉṟavaiyē araciyal, poruḷātāram paṟṟi avvappoḻutu nilavi varum, palvēṟu “icaṅkaḷum” (isms) avai paṟṟiya nampikkaikaḷum ākum.

2. cintu nākarikac ciṉṉaṅkaḷ 1921 liruntu akaḻvāyvil veḷippaṭat toṭaṅkiṉa. āyiṉum ataṟkumuṉṉarē (ēṉ 19-m nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ iṭaiyiliruntē) kālṭuvel, maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ uḷḷiṭṭa aṟiñar palar āriyar ki.mu.1500 ai oṭṭi intiyāvukkuḷ nuḻaiyumuṉṉar ciṟanta tamiḻiya / tirāviṭa nākarikam intiyā muḻuvatum paraviyiruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉṟum piṟkāla intiyar vāḻviyal, camayam uṭpaṭa, tamiḻiya nākarikattai aṭippaṭaiyākak koṇṭatē eṉṟa karuttai terivittiruntatu paṟṟiya muḻuvivaraṅkaḷ piṟa iyal kaḷilum uḷḷaṉa.

3. cintu nākarika camayattaip paṟṟi 2001 il ār.ci.taṇṭā ‘hārappāvil tōṉṟiya intu camayam’ Harappan origin of Hindiusm  mukkiyamāṉa nūlākum intiyāvil iṉṟum vaḻaṅkivarum intu camayattaip pōṉṟa camayamē cintu nākarikattilum vaḻaṅkiyatu eṉṟa carjāṉ mārṣalkaruttai iṉṟu aṉaittu aṟiñarum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. (“Their religiion is so characteristically Indian, as hardly to be distinguished from the still living Hinduism”) 

4. cintu nākarikattil cumēriyā, ekiptu nākarikaṅkaḷil iruntatu pōṉṟa periya kaṭṭumāṉak kōyilkaḷ illai eṉiṉum caṅkakālat tamiḻakattil iruntatupōla marattaṭi vaḻipāṭu pōṉṟavai paravalāka iruntirukkum. vīṭukaḷilum kaṭavuḷ uruvaṅkaḷ, takaṭukaḷ, tāyattukkaḷ pōṉṟavai vaḻipāṭṭukkup payaṉpaṭṭirukkalām. muttiraikaḷil uḷḷa uruvaṅkaḷiliruntu aracamaram pōṉṟa maravaḻipāṭu erutu, yāṉai
pōṉṟa vilaṅkukaḷ vaḻipāṭu, peṇ teyva vaḻipāṭu pōṉṟavai iruntatu uṇarappaṭukiṟatu. ‘pacupati’ muttirai (mokañcotarō: 420) yōki uruvil amainta civaṉaik kuṟippatu eṉpatu mārṣal karuttu. māṟāka atu peṇteyvam eṉṟu karutupavarkaḷ ec.pi.calli vāṉ (1964), ē.hiltepeytal (1978), cupaṅkaṇā ātrē (1987) ākiyōr āvar; ikkaruttu potuvāka ēṟkappaṭavillai. āṭavallāṉ paṭimattiṟku muṉṉōṭiyeṉak karutattakka cilai hārappāvil kiṭaittai mēlē kaṇṭōm. liṅka vaṭivaṅkaḷ palavum kiṭait tuḷḷaṉa. muttiraikaḷil oṉṟu camaṇa riṣapa tēvaraik kuṟippa tākalām - (ār.pi.cantā: māṭarṉ riviyū , ākasṭu 1932). cakkaramum ‘cuvattikamum’ ñāyiṟṟaik kuṟippatākaliṉ ñāyiṟu vaḻipāṭum irun tirukkum eṉpar cilar. kiṭaittuḷḷa ciṟu ciṟu patumaikaḷil cila nērttik kaṭaṉākak kōyilkaḷukku vaḻaṅkappaṭṭavaiyeṉṟum karutu kiṉṟaṉar. teppakkuḷam, nīrāṭal mutaliyavaṟṟiṉ mutaṉmaiyi liruntum piṟkāla intucamaya muṉṉōṭi in nākarikattil kāṇak kiṭakkiṟatu.

5. tirāviṭariṉ tāyt teyvam koṟṟavai, paḻaiyōḷ, kāṉamar celvi, kāṭu kiḻāḷ, kaṭalkeḻucelvi eṉap pala peyarkaḷāl aḻaikkappaṭṭāḷ. avaḷē cēyōṉākiya murukaṉiṉ tāy (perum pāṇāṟṟup paṭai : 457) hārappā nākarika tāyt teyvamum koṟ ṟavaiyiṉ vaṭivē eṉpatu pi.el. cāmi karuttu. lōtalil vaḻipaṭṭatāka aṟiyappaṭum ‘vaṉavāci koṭṭarimātā’ vaṭivam kāṉamar celviyē; ellaiyammaṉ (ellammaṉ) maṟu uruvamē rēṇukā eṉpatum avar karuttu. ivvaṉaittup peṇ teyvaṅkaḷiṉ iṇaippiṉ kāraṇa mākavē piṟkālattil lajja kauri pōṉṟa peṇ teyvaṅkaḷ vaṭanāṭṭil uruvāyiṉar eṉkiṟār avar (Journal of Tamil Studies December 1992.)  ‘mo. 430’ muttirai pōṉṟavaṟṟil kāṇappaṭum ēḻukaṉṉiyar vaṭivaṅkaḷ kārttikaip peṇkaḷaik kuṟikkalām eṉpar parpōlā (South Asian Archaeology, 1987)  vaṭa intiyar tāyt teyvattai Hoi (She happened : āy?) eṉṟa peyaril vaḻipaṭukiṉṟaṉar eṉpar taṇṭā (pak. 190)

6. intiyāvil camaya vaḷarcciyil kuṟippiṭattakkatu oṉṟu uṇṭu. uyirkaḷ tam viṉaip payaṉukkēṟpa mīṇṭum mīṇṭum piṟavi yeṭukkum eṉpatu pautta matattiṉ mukkiya koḷkai. puttamatam tōṉṟiya kālattil tōṉṟiya camaṇamum ācīvakamum ak koḷkai yuṭaiyavaiyē. piṟavit taḷaiyiliruntu viṭupaṭuvatu camayattiṉ kuṟikkōḷ eṉpatu ammataṅkaḷiṉ koḷkai. immūṉṟu camayaṅ kaḷum, cāṅkiya - yōkā kōṭpāṭukaḷum maṭṭumaṉṟi, vētāntak koḷkaiyum āriyarallātavaruṭaiyavai eṉpatu vallunar karuttu:-

ālparṭ cuvaiṭcar: ‘Vedanta is Brahminized Sankhya’ 
heyṉric jimmar‘Sankhya - Yoga, Jainism and early Buddhism
represent the thinking of non - Aryan people of India’. 

inta maṟupiṟavik koḷkaiyai car villiyam jōṉs pārāṭṭiyuḷḷār. tattamatu kaṭavuḷ coṉṉatākak kūṟappaṭum camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷai mīṟum maṉitar mīḷā narakattil eṉṟeṉṟum muṭiviṉṟic cittira vataikkuṭpaṭuvar eṉṟa kurūramāṉa kōṭpāṭṭaik koṇṭavai (kiṟittuvam, iculām pōṉṟa) cemittiya mataṅkaḷ; akkōṭpāṭṭai viṭa intiya camayaṅkaḷiṉ maṟu piṟavik koḷkai evvaḷavō aṟivu pūrvamāṉatu; kaṭavuḷ uṇarccikkukantatu; tīmaiyiṉpāṟ cellātu maṉitaraik taṭukkavallatu eṉa jōṉs kūṟiyuḷḷār.

7. jē.el. pirākkiṅṭaṉ itupaṟṟik kūṟuvatu ( The Sacred Thread); 

viṉaip payaṉai nampuvatu eṉpatu maṉitaṉ cuyamuyaṟciyaik kaiviṭṭu ‘naṭappatu naṭakkaṭṭum’ eṉa vāḷā irukkac ceyyum nilai (fatalism)  eṉap potuvākak karutuvatu aṟiyāmaiyākum. ‘muntaic ceyalkaḷāl iṉṟaiya nilai eṟpaṭṭuḷḷatu. iṉṟu nām ceyyum ceyalkaḷ varuṅkālattai nirṇayikkum’ eṉṟu collum poḻutu ‘maṉitaṉiṉ iṉṟaiya nilai taṉ ceyalāl ēṟpaṭṭatu; varuṅ kālattaiyum taṉ ceyalāl nirṇayikkalām” eṉṟu aṟivuṟuttuva tākavē koḷḷa vēṇṭum.

paḻaiya upaniṭataṅkaḷil maṟupiṟavik koḷkai putumaiyāṉa tākavum cilarukku maṭṭum terinta maṟai poruḷākavum kuṟippiṭappaṭṭu iruntatu. iruntum piṉṉar akkoḷkai putta camaṇa camayaṅkaḷ vaḻiyāka viraintu aṉaivarālum piṉpaṟṟap paṭṭatu evvāṟu? enta makkaḷ, camūkattiṉariṭam iruntu pauttar tōṉṟiṉārō avarka ḷiṭaiyē ak koḷkai paravalākat toṉṟu toṭṭu vaḻaṅki vanta koḷkaiyāka atu iruntirukkavēṇṭum. eṉavētāṉ atu viraivil aṉaittu makkaḷiṉ kōṭpāṭāka ēṟṟuk koḷḷappaṭṭatu

8. “vaṭamēṟkiliruntu nuḻainta āriyarkaḷāl makatam mutaliya kīḻtticai nāṭukaḷilirunta āriyarallāta mēṉmakkaḷ muḻumaiyāka iṭam peyarntilar. attakaiya mēṉmakkaḷ kuṭumpaṅkaḷ pala toṭarntu iruntaṉa. āriya āḷum kuṭumpaṅkaḷ valuviḻantu vīḻat toṭaṅkiya piṉ muntaiya uḷḷūr āḷum kuṭumpaṅkaḷ mīṇṭum valuppeṟṟaṉa. eṭuttukkāṭṭāka cantirakupta mauriyaṉ ittakaiya kuṭumpattaic cārntavaṉ. puttarum avvāṟē” eṉṟu heyṉric cimmar (Heinrich Zimmer: The Philosophies of India; 1951) ceppiyuḷḷataiyum karutuka.

9. intu camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷil aṭippaṭaiyākavum ciṟantaṉa vākavum uḷḷavaiyellām vaṭanāṭṭil paravalāka 3000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉ vāḻnta tamiḻ mēṉmakkaḷiṭamiruntu peṟappaṭṭavai eṉpatai yum avaṟṟai āriyaruṭaiyaṉavākak koḷvatu aṟiyāmai eṉpataiyum maṟaimalai aṭikaḷ tamiḻar nākarikam allatu vēḷāḷar yār? (1923); māṇikkavācakar varalāṟum kālamum (1930); tamiḻar matam (1941) pōṉṟa nūlkaḷil niṟuviyuḷḷār. kā.cuppiramaṇiya piḷḷaitamiḻarcamayam (1940) tēvanēyap pāvāṇartamiḻar matam (1972) ākiyōrukkum aṭikaḷ karuttu uṭaṉpāṭēyākum.

10.rik vēta kālattiliruntē (ki.mu.1000) “kuṭi, kolai malinta āriya vēḷviyiṉ tīviṉaip peṟṟiyuñ ciṟu teyva vaṇakkac ciṟumaiyun tēṟṟi aṉpum aruḷum perukac ceyta tamiḻc cāṉṟōrum uḷar. avar aruḷic ceytaṉavē muttī vēḷviyum uruttira vaḻipāṭuñ ciṟanteṭuttuk kūṟum irukku, ecur, cāma, atarva vētap patikaṅkaḷ cila palavum; uṇmai vaḻā upaniṭataṅ kaḷum; cāṅkiya, yōka, vaicēṭika, naiyāyika, vētānta cūttiraṅ kaḷum; maṟcam, vāyu mutalāṉa cila purāṇaṅkaḷum; pauṭkaram, mirukēn tiram mutalāṉa cila civākamaṅkaḷum piṟavumām’ eṉpatu aṭikaḷ 1930 il eḻutiya muṭivu. im muṭivukkāṉa eṭuttukkāṭṭukaḷāka maṟai malai aṭikaḷ tantuḷḷa palavaṟṟuḷ mutaṉmaiyāṉa cila varumāṟu:-

‘irukku vētattiṟ ‘paratar’ mutaliya peyarkaḷāl vaḻaṅkap paṭṭa paṇṭait tamiḻ naṉmakkaḷē (“paratar’ eṉṉum peyariliruntē ‘pāratam” uruvāṉatu). ñāyiṟu, tiṅkaḷ, tī eṉṉum muttī vaṭivil vaittu muḻumutaṟ kaṭavuḷāṉa civapirāṉai vaḻipaṭum nuṭpa muṟaiyaik kaṇṭōrāvar’ (1930)

‘vicuvāmittirar eṉṉum tamiḻaraca muṉivarāl ceytu cērkkappaṭṭa irukku vēta mūṉṟām maṇṭilattil uḷḷa kāyattiri mantiramāṉatu ñāyiṟṟu maṇṭilattiṉ kaṇ muḷaittu viḷaṅkum parkkaṉ eṉṉum peyaruṭaiya civapirāṉ mēṟṟāy viḷaṅkukiṟatu’ (1923)

“kollā aṟattai mutaṉmutaṟ kaṇṭaṟintu ataṟ kēṟpat tamatu immai vāḻkkaiyai naṭattiṉavarkaḷ tamiḻaril mēṉmakkaḷāy irunta oru perum pakutiyārē ... maṟṟai makkaṭ piriviṉariluḷ enta mēlōrum ivvarumaruntaṉṉa uṇmaiyiṉaik kaṇṭaṟintilar ... vaṭakkilirunta camaṇa, cākkiyar, cāṅkiyar, yōkar mutalāyiṉāruṅ kollā vaṟattiṉai viṭāppiṭiyāyk koṇṭu oḻukiṉaralla rō veṉil; camaṇa cākkiyar mutalāṉa avarellām āriyar varutaṟku muṉṉamē imayamalai varaiyiṟ paravi nākarikattiṟ ciṟantu uyir vāḻnta vaṭanāṭṭuta tamiḻ mēṉmakkaḷiṉ marapil vantavarē’ (1940)

11. tamatu cāti vēṟṟumaiyum pōliccaivarum (1923) nūlil maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ virivāka viḷakkum piṉvarum ceytikaḷ ivviyal, aṭutta iyal iraṇṭiṉ poruḷkaḷukkumē toṭarpuṭai yaṉavākum. eṉiṉum vacati karuti ivviyalilēyē tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. (nuṇmāṇuḻaipulamikka akkaruttukaḷ vēta / camaskiruta moḻi, paṇpāṭṭu āyvāḷarkaḷ (innāṭṭavarum cari, mēṉāṭṭavarum cari) yārālum ivvaḷavu teḷivāka terivikkappaṭavillai):

nālvakai makkaṭpiriviṉariṉ peyarai avvavar toḻil veṟupāṭupaṟṟi eṭuttōtum patikam ip puruṭacūtta mantirattait tavira vēṟētum irukku vētattil eṅkuṅ kāṇappaṭāmai yāṉum, oṉpatāñ ceyyuḷil irukku, ecur cāmam eṉṉum mūṉṟu vētaṅkaḷiṉ peyarai eṭuttuc collum ip puruṭa cūttam am mūṉṟuvētaṅ kaḷun tōṉṟiya piṉṉaṉṟi muṉṉiruntatu ākāmai yāṉum, nālvēṟu makkaṭ pākupāṭum ataṉai yeṭuttuk kūṟum puruṭacūttamumirukku, ecur ,cāmam eṉṉum mūṉṟu vētaṅkaḷum iyaṟṟappaṭṭakālattil iruntaṉa alla eṉpatum uṇarntu koḷḷal vēṇṭum.

iṉi, māpāratap pōril muṉaintu niṉṟa kurukula pāñcāla aracar kaḷaiyum, avar vaḻiyil vanta jaṉakaṉ ajātacatturu, jaṉamējaya, parikṣit, ivaṟku āciriyaṉākiya vaicampāyaṉaṉ mutaliyōrai yum eṭuttōtumkiruṣṇa cuklayajur vētamum ataṉōṭu oru kālattākiya cāma vētamum am māpāratappōr nikaḻntataṟkuppiṉ ākkap paṭṭaṉavātal teḷiyappaṭum. ivviraṇṭu vētaṅkaḷ iyaṟṟappaṭṭa kālattaiyaṭuttē, av vētaṅkaḷukku viḷakka uraikaḷāy eḻunta ‘pirāmaṇaṅ’kaḷum avaṟṟaiyaṭutta vēḷvi vēṭṭalai maṟuttu muḻu mutaṟkaṭavuḷiṉ meym maiyaip pulappaṭuttavum paṉṉiraṇṭu paḻaiya upaniṭataṅkaḷum ākkappaṭṭaṉa. ivaikaḷuṇṭāṉa kālattiluṅ kūṭap, piṟappaḷavil cātivēṟupāṭu collappaṭa villai. cukla yacur vētattiṉ muppatām iyaliṟ pala vēṟu toḻilkaḷaic ceyyum palvēṟu makkaṭkūṭṭattiṉar peyarkaḷ maṭṭumē eṭutturaikkappaṭ ṭirukkiṉṟaṉa; iṅṅaṉamē taittirīya pirāmaṇattiluñ collap paṭṭiruk kiṉṟaṉa; āṉāl, avaṟṟuṭ cātivēṟupāṭukaḷum, avaṟṟiṉ uyarpu iḻipukaḷum pēcappaṭa villai. ataṟku oru cāṉṟu eṭuttuk kāṭṭutum: muṉṉoru kāl caracuvati yāṟṟaṅkaraiyilē iruṭikaḷ oru vēḷvi vēṭkalāyiṉar. appōtu ‘illūṣai’ eṉṉum ōr aṭimaip peṇṇiṉ putalvarāṉa ‘kavaṣā’ eṉpavar av vēḷvikku vantiruntaṉar. avarkaḷ avarai ōr aṭimaiyiṉ piḷḷai eṉpatu paṟṟi ikaḻntu, av vēḷvikkaḷattiṉiṉṟun turatta, avar ceṉṟa iṭattiṟkē avyāṟṟu nīr cellumāṟu kaṭavuḷ aruḷceytaṉar; ataṉaiyuṇarnta iruṭikaḷ avaraiyikaḻnta tamatu aṟiyāmaikku varuntip piṉṉar avarai oru ciṟanta iruṭiyāka ēṟṟu vaṇaṅkiṉar; eṉṟu aitrēyapirāmaṇam (2, 19) pukalukiṉṟatu. ik ‘kavaṣā’ eṉpavarē irukku vētattiṉ pattām maṇṭilattiṟ pala patikaṅkaḷai iyaṟṟi yirukkiṉṟaṉar. ivvāṟṟāṟpirāmaṇaṅkaḷ eḻutappaṭṭa kālattilum cāti vēṟṟumai nilaipeṟavillai yeṉpatu pulaṉākiṉṟataṉṟō?

iṉṉum, pala vēḷvikaḷaic ceytu muṭittavaṉum, av vēḷvikaḷilmuṉivararkkum piṟarkkum ērāḷamāṉa naṉkoṭai vaḻaṅkiṉavaṉum, vicuvā mittira muṉivaraip pātukāttavaṉum, irukku vēta ēḻām maṇṭilattiṉ 18 ām patikattiṟ pārāṭṭap paṭṭavaṉu māṉa ‘paijavaṉaṉ’ eṉpāṉ oru cūttiraṉē eṉṟu māpāratam cāntiparvam 2304 ām ceyyuḷiliruntu nuvalkiṉṟatu.

iṉṉum,irukkuvētap patikaṅkaḷai ākkiya muṉivarar palarum araca vakuppiṉaiyum vaṇika vakuppiṉaiyuñ cērntōr āvareṉṟu maṟcapurāṇattiṉ 132ām iyal vakutturaikkiṉṟatu. irukku vēta ttiṉ mūṉṟām maṇṭilattai ākkiya vicuvāmittirarum, maṟṟai maṇṭilaṅkaḷiṟ patikaṅkaḷ palavaṟṟai ākkiya vaivacuva tamaṉu, iṭaṉ, purūravar mutalāyiṉārum araca vakuppiṉarē āvar; palantar, vantiyar, caṅkīrtti mutaliyōr vaiciya vakuppiṉa rāvar.irukku vēta ttiṉ, mutal maṇṭilattuḷḷa 100 ām patikattai iyaṟṟiya rijrācuvar, amparīṣar, cakatēvar, payamā nar, curātar mutalāṉa iruṭikaḷ aivarum viruṣākir aracaṉ putalvarāvar eṉṟu irukku vētattiṉ anuk kiramaṇikaiyē pukalkiṉṟatu. iṅṅaṉamē, av virukkuvētattiṉ 6 āvatu maṇṭilattiluḷḷa 15 ām patikattaic ceyta vītahavyarum, 10 āvatu maṇṭilattu 9 ām patikattai ākkiya amparīṭar makaṉāṉa cintut vīparum, ataṉ 75 ām patikattai iyaṟṟiya piriya mētar makaṉāṉa cintukṣittarum, ataṉ 179 ām patikattaip pāṭiya ucīnarariṉ makaṉ cipiyum, kācimaṉṉaṉ tivōtācaṉ makaṉ piratarttaṉarum, ataṉ 98 ām patikattaip pukaṉṟa cantanuviṉ makaṉ tēvāpiyum ellām araca iṉattaic cērn tōrēyāvar. ivvāṟē iṉṉum palar uḷar.

mēṟcolliya irukkuvētap patikaṅkaḷai ākkiya cattiriya vaiciyar eṉpār tamiḻa vēḷāḷa vakuppiṉarē yāvar. yāṅṅaṉa meṉiṟ kūṟutum: mēṟkāṭṭiya rijrācuvar mutalāṉa araca muṉivar aivarum cērntiyaṟṟiya patikattiṉ 12 ām ceyyuḷil ‘cōmaṉ’ eṉṉuṅ kaṭavuḷ aintu iṉattāraik kāppavarākac collappaṭu kiṉṟaṉar. ic ‘cōmaṉ’ eṉṉuñ col ‘ca umā’ eṉṉum iru moḻip puṇarcciyāṟ ṟōṉṟi ‘umaiyōṭu kūṭiṉavar’ eṉṟu poruḷpaṭum eṉa atarvaciras upaniṭata uraiyilē viḷakkappaṭṭiruttaliṉ, ac col civapirāṉ mēṟṟātalum, ‘cōmanātam’ eṉṉum vaṭanāṭṭuc civa pirāṉ tirukkōyil ap peyarpeṟṟiruttaliṉ aḵtu ataṟkup piṉṉum oru cāṉṟātalum naṉku viḷaṅkum. iṉic civapirāṉāṟ kākkappaṭum aivakai iṉattiṉar āvār turuvācar kaḷum yatukkaḷum aṇukkaḷum turukiyarkaḷum pūrukkaḷum eṉa aivar avareṉṟu kiripit eṉṉum āciriyar naṉkeṭuttuk kāṭṭiyirukkiṉṟār. (rik vēta āṅkila vaṭivam, I pak. 10) iv vaivakai iṉattiṉarum tamiḻmakkaṭ piriviṉarē yāva reṉṟu rākociṉ eṉṉum varalāṟṟu nūlāciriyar tām āḻntārāynteḻutiya vēta intiyā (pak. 323-9) eṉṉum nūlil virittu viḷakkiyirukkiṉṟār. 2 vaṭakkiruntu vantu intiya nāṭṭuḷ nuḻainta āriya makkaḷai etirtta pattu aracar kaḷukkut talaivarāy niṉṟōr pūrukkaḷum, avarkkut tuṇaivarāy añcāneñciṉarāy nilaviya paratarkaḷum āvar. it tamiḻa aracar patiṉmaruṭ civar (caivar) viṣāniyar (vaiṣṇavar) eṉpār iruvarum cērttuc collap paṭukiṉṟaṉar (rikvētam 7, 18). ākavē, attuṇaip paḻaiya kālattilēyē tamiḻaruṭ civa pirāṉai vaṇaṅkuñ caivarum, viṣṇuvai vaṇaṅkum vaiṣṇavarum valiyarāyiruntu āriyarai etirttu niṉṟamai pulaṉākum. paḻaiya tamiḻ makkaḷuṭ pōr valimaiyil mikac ciṟantuniṉṟa paratar tam kuṭiyiṟ ṟōṉṟiya aracarkaḷē iv intiya nāṭu muḻumaiyum āṇṭuvantaṉar; atupaṟṟiyē, avarkaḷiṉ ceṅkōl niḻalil vantu vaikiya āriya makkaḷ iv intiya nāṭṭaip “pārata varṣam’ eṉṟu aḻaippārāyiṉar. kumari nāṭu kaṭalkoṇṭapiṉ aṅkirunta tamiḻarkaḷē vaṭanāṭu varaiyiṟ ceṉṟu āṅkāṅkuk kuṭiyēṟip pattuvakai araciyalai nāṭṭi ṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷuḷ vaṭamaturaiyil aracāṇṭa yatu kulattavarkaḷē piṉṉark kaṇṇaṉait talai vaṉāyk koṇṭu tuvārakaiyil vantu kuṭiyēṟiṉar. av yatu kulattavaril oru pakutiyārē peyarttun tamiḻnāṭṭiṟ pōntu erumaiyūriṟ kuṭiyēṟiṉar(R.C.Dutt:  ai pak. 19) erumaiyūriṟ kuṭiyēṟiya yatukulattavarākiya vēḷirkkut talaivaṉē puṟanāṉūṟṟiṟ kūṟappaṭum iruṅkō vēḷ eṉṉum maṉṉaṉ āvāṉ. ivvāṟu ivar pōṟ ṟamiḻ nāṭṭiṉum piṟanāṭukaḷiṉum an nāḷiṟ paraviyirunta vēḷāḷa maṉṉarkaḷ varalāṟukaḷai uraikkap pukiṉ itu mika viriyum. it tamiḻa vēḷāḷa aracarkaḷaiyē paḻaiya vaṭamoḻivētaṅkaḷum upaniṭataṅkaḷum ‘rājanyar’, ‘cattiriyar’ eṉṉum peyarkaḷāṟ kuṟippavāyiṉa eṉpataṟku, maṉu tirāviṭarkaḷaic ‘cattiriyar’ eṉṟu kūṟutalē (10, 43, 44) cāṉṟām. tamiḻa vēḷāḷaril uḻavuttoḻil ceytārum, uḻavut toḻilāṟ peṟṟa paṇṭaṅkaḷaik koṇṭu viṟṟārumē vaṭanūlkaḷil ‘vaiciyar’ eṉṟu nuvalappaṭuvārā yiṉar. ivvāṟu kāṭṭappaṭṭa cattiriyarum vaiciyarumākiya tamiḻa vēḷāḷarē toṉṟu toṭṭuc civapirāṉai vaṇaṅki varupavarāy iruttaliṉ, am makkaṭpiriviṉar aivaraiyuṅ kāppavar ‘cōmaṉ’ eṉṉum civapirāṉāka irukku vētam pukaluvatāyiṟṟeṉka. eṉavē, irukku vētattiṟ palappala patikaṅkaḷ iyaṟṟiya cattiriya vaiciyarkaḷ aṉaivarum tamiḻa vēḷāḷarkaḷē yātalait teṟṟeṉa uṇarntukoḷka.

ivvāṟu cattiriya vaiciyarākiya vēḷāḷarkaḷum ivar tamakkuk kuṟṟēvaṟṟoḻil purivārāṉa cūttirarum, maṟṟai āriyarum kalantu vētaṅkaḷaiyum pirāmaṇaṅkaḷaiyum paṉṉiraṇṭu upaniṭataṅkaḷai yum ākkivaitta kālattiṟ, piṟappaḷavil tamakkuḷ uyarvu tāḻvu kāṇātu, uyirvāḻkkaikku iṉṟi yamaiyāta nālvēṟu toḻilkaḷaiyam ceytu koṇṭu uṇṇal kalattalkaḷil ētoru vēṟupāṭu miṉṟi orumittu vāḻntaṉarākaliṉ, ak kālattiṟ piṟappaḷavil uyarvu tāḻvu koḷḷum cāti vēṟṟumai ciṟitumillaiyeṉṟu teḷika. itaṉā laṉṟōvāyupurāṇamum (8, 190-3) ‘tīyavaṉukku, maṉattūymai iḻantavaṉukku vētaṅkaḷum avaṟṟiṉ caṭaṅkukaḷum vēḷvikaḷum paṭṭiṉi kiṭattalum teṉ pulattār kaṭaṉiṟuttal mutalāṉa viṉaikaḷum payaṉait tarukiṉṟila; akattē iḻinta iyaṟkaiyuṭaiyavaṉāyiruppōṉ puṟattē ettuṇai muyaṟciyōṭu caṭaṅkukaḷaic ceytāṉāyiṉum, avai ciṟitum payaṉpaṭā. oruvaṉ aḻukku neñcat tōṭu taṉakkuḷḷa poruḷ muḻutuṅ kāṭuttāṉēṉum, ataṉāl naṉmai eytamāṭṭāṉ; ātalāl nalla taṉmaiyē uyarccikku ētuvākum,” eṉṟu kūṟuvatāyiṟṟu.

iṉi, mūṉṟu vētaṅkaḷukkum piṟpaṭṭatākiya puruṭa cūttat tilum, ataṟkum piṟpaṭṭatākiya piruka tāraṇiyaka upaniṭatat tilum (1, 4, 15) nālvakaic cātippeyar kāṇappaṭiṉum, muṉṉaiyatil an nāṟ cātiyiṉarum oru muḻumutaṟkaṭavuḷiṉ piḷḷaikaḷātalum, piṉṉaiyatil annāṟcātiyiṉariṭattum iṟaivaṉ niṟaintu viḷaṅkutalum naṉku vaṟpuṟuttu uraikkap paṭutaliṉ, in nūlkaḷ tōṉṟiya kālattil nālvakaic cātikaḷ iruntamai peṟappaṭiṉum, avaruḷ uyarvu tāḻvukaḷ iruntamai pulaṉāka villai.

pirāmaṇaṅkaḷ tōṉṟuvataṟku muṟpaṭṭa vētakālat til pūṇūl aṇiyum vaḻakkaṅ kāṇappaṭāmaiyāl, atupaṟṟi yuṇṭāṉa cāti vēṟṟumaiyum aññāṉṟu iruntatillai. catapatapirāmaṇat tilum (2, 4, 2), kauṣītaki upaniṭatattilumē (2, 7) pūṇūl aṇiyuṅ kuṟippu mutaṉmutaṟ kāṇappaṭukiṉṟatu. ivviraṇ ṭiluṅkūṭap pakalavaṉ kīḻpāl eḻumpōtu avaṉai vaṇaṅkuṅ kālaippoḻutilum, vēḷvi vēṭkum poḻutilumē māntar pūṇūl aṇintu maṟṟaik kālaṅkaḷil ataṉaik kaḻaṟṟiviṭum vaḻakkam naṉku terintōtap paṭṭamaiyāl, in nūlkaḷ uṇṭāṉa kālattilum cātivēṟṟumai yiṉ poruṭṭup pūṇūl aṇiyappaṭavillaiyeṉpatum, iṟaivaṉai vaḻipaṭun toḻiliṟ pukuntiruppōr attoḻiliṟ pukātār tammai aṇukāmal vilakippōtaṟku or aṭaiyāḷa mākavē ataṉai attoḻil iyaṟṟum nēraṅkaḷil maṭṭum aṇintiruntaṉareṉpatum iṉitu viḷaṅkum.



āriyar maṭṭumaṉṟi āriyarallāta piṟarum (tirāviṭarum) rikvēta kālattilēyē pirāmaṇarāka, riṣikaḷāka ēṟkappaṭṭaṉar eṉpatu tāmōtara tarmānanta kōcampi (1950-51 On the Origin of the Brahmin gotras Jounal of the Bombay Branch of the Royal Asiatic Society  26.21-80l) uṭaiya muṭivum ākum. attakai tirāviṭa riṣikaḷ tāṅkaḷ enta āriya aracaṉiṭam cērntukoṇṭaṉarō avaṉ kōttirattaiyē tamatu kōttiramākak koṇṭaṉar (piṟkālattil itait talaikīḻāka māṟṟi kuruvāṉa pirāmaṇaṉiṉ kōttiramē aracaṉukkum eṉṟu puṉaintu koṇṭaṉar.) uṇmainilai ituvākaiyāltāṉ intiyāvil palapakutikaḷilum uḷḷa pirāmaṇarum uṭalamaippu, tōṟṟam ākiya uṭaliyaṟ kūṟukaḷil perumaḷavu māṟupaṭṭu uḷḷatōṭu, oru mānila pirāmaṇar iṉṉoru mānila pirāmaṇaruṭaṉ maṇa uṟavu koḷḷāta nilaimaiyum, oru mānilattiṟkuḷḷum uḷḷa palavakai pirāmaṇac cātiyiṉar tattamakkuḷ maṇa uṟavu koḷḷāta nilaimaiyum uḷḷateṉpar kōcampi.

“My thesis is that, specifically as regards some important Brahmins, the gotra system is adopted by small groups of pre-Ksatriya and pre - Aryan people from Aryan invaders; as these groups take the functions of priesthood, they are most logically assigned to the patriarchal clan-group of those for whom they officiate. They consequently acquire the same gotra; only afterwards does the rule become its opposite, when the Vedic Kshatrias have died out by the rise of settlements and the emergence of other warriors of obscure origin who fight their way to the top. At that stage, it becomes quite possible to assign to these newcomers, the same gotra as that of the priests, who have maintained a continuity of tradition and acquired a monopoly of scripture by long and arduous study. I do not mean to imply that all gotras, or even all Brahmin gotras originated in this way.

xxx. gotra lists could not be closed, and newcomers were obviously being recruited into the ranks of the priesthood. The Nagara Brahmins of Gujarat are supposed to be medieval immigrants. If the institution of marriage were so stricty bounded by caste and gotra rules, it would be difficult to explain the strong racial heterogeneity of Brahmins in India, as well as the existence of endogamous regional units within them (amounting to sub - castes) which have no basis in scripture)
- D.D. Kosambi, 1950 - 51 

12. aṭikaḷāriṉ mēṟcoṉṉa karuttukkaḷiṉ karuvaip piṉvaru māṟu kā. appātturaiyār 1956 il “1800 āṇṭukaṭku muṟpaṭṭa tamiḻakam ” tamiḻākkattiṉ aṭik kuṟippil) curuṅkak kuṟittār:

“tamiḻar paṇṭaiyac camaya vāḻvu iṉṟaiya pala camayaṅkaḷiṉ uruvākāmūlak karu mutalait taṉṉiṭa mākak koṇṭa vāḻvē yākum. atil ellāc camayamūlamum kāṇalām... putta, camaṇa, caiva, vaiṇavak karuttukkaḷ uruvākumuṉ, ac camayap peyarkaḷ vēṟu vēṟākat tamiḻar neṟiyiṉiṉṟu piriyu muṉ, āriyar iṭainiṉṟu tamiḻariṉ oruneṟiyaip palavāṟu pirikkumuṉ avvellā neṟi kaḷukkum vaḻikāṭṭiya mika muṟpaṭṭa kālat tamiḻ aṟivar marapucārnta aṟiñaruḷ oruvarē tiruvaḷḷuvar eṉpatu uyttuṇarattakka uṇmaiyākum.”

13. maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ karuttukkaḷaip pōṉṟē vēṟupala aṟiñar terivittuḷḷa icainta karuttukkaḷ varumāṟu:-

(i) “namakkuk kiṭaikkum takavalkaḷellām orupakkattiliruntu kiṭaikkum (pirāmaṇac cārpāṉa) takavalkaḷē. muḻut takavalkaḷum kiṭṭiyiruntāl intiyar camayam tirāviṭar camayamē (iṅkuvanta āriyarkaḷ cintaṉaikkēṟpac ceyyappaṭṭa cila māṟṟaṅkaḷukkuṭ paṭṭu) eṉpatai uṇarntu iruppōm.

“pirāmaṇac camutāyattōṭu muḻumaiyāka oppiṭakkūṭiya amaippu ulakil vēṟu enta nāṭṭilum illai. kattōlikkap pātirikaḷaip pōlavō, mukamatiya mullākkaḷaip pōlavō oru kuṟippiṭṭa kōṭpāṭṭaik kiḷippiḷḷai pōla kālā kālamum tūkkippiṭittu valiyuṟutti varuvatalla pirāmaṇar taṉmai. intiyā vil uruvākum allatu nuḻaiyum entac camayattaiyum kōṭ pāṭṭaiyum kālattukkut takka māṟṟaṅkaḷuṭaṉ taṅkaḷ talaimaiyil ceyalpaṭuttak karutum cintaṉaiyāḷa mēṭṭukkuṭip paramparai tāṅkaḷtām eṉpavar avarkaḷ”.

“pirāmaṇaruṭaiya nōkkam ‘kuṟippāka inta camayattaittāṉ parappa vēṇṭum’ eṉpatāka eṉṟum iruntatillai. intiyāvil paravum entac camayamāyiṉum cari ataṉai vaḻinaṭattik kaiyāḷupavarkaḷ tāṅkaḷāka irukkavēṇṭum eṉpatē avarkaḷ kuṟikkōḷ”. – car cārlas eliyaṭBuddhism and Hinduism’ vol 2; p.9

“Were our knowledge less one-sided, we might see that it would be more correct to describe Indian religion as Dravidian religion stimulated and modified by the ideas of Aryan invaders”

“The Brahmans are an interesting social phenomenon without exact parallel elsewhere. They are not like the Catholic or Muslim clergy, a priesthood pledged to support certain doctrines, but an intellectual, hereditary aristocracy who claim to direct the thought of India, whatever forms it may take.” 

‘The guiding priciples of the Brahmins have alway been not so much that they have a particular creed to enforce, as that whatever the creed of India, they must be its ministers’ 

(ii) viṣṇu teṉṉāṭṭil tirāviṭar vaḻipaṭṭa oru teyvam. piṉṉar āriyar viṣṇuvait tam teyvamākavum ēṟṟuk koṇṭaṉar (jē. pirislaski 1934-35 The name of the God Visnu and the Krsna legend” Quarterly Journal of the Mythic Society; pp 39-48. 

(iii) rikvēta kālattilēyē āriyar paṇpāṭu ‘muḻu āriyat’ taṉmaiyai iḻantuviṭṭatu eṉpatai avvēta 8 ām maṇṭalatti liruntu uṉṉikkalām. āriyarallātār paṇpāṭu rikvētattil uḷḷatu - atuvum kuṟippākap perumaḷavukku eṭṭām maṇṭalattil. (kuññaṉ rājā 1940 kē.vi. raṅkacāmi aiyaṅkār pārāṭṭu malar)

The presence of the eighth book leads one to the hypothesis that even at the time of the Rig Veda, the Aryan culture had ceased to be purely an Aryan one. There is a good deal of non-Aryan element in the Rig Veda, mostly in the eighth book. 

(iv) iltikō pukās karuttu: rikvēta riṣikaḷil perum pālōr āriyarallātārākattāṉ iruntirukkavēṇṭum. South Asian Archaelogy 1981 (Cambridge, 1984)

(v) ‘intiyāvil vētakālattukku muntaiya kālac camayam perumaḷavukku hārappā nākarikattil vaḻaṅkiya tirāviṭacamaya mākavē iruntatu. accamayattai mutalil kaṭaippiṭikkat toṭaṅ kiyavarkaḷ ‘rikvēta ’ āriyarukku muṉṉarē cintuppakutikku vantu viṭṭa āriyarkaḷ (piṟṟai ‘rikvēta ’ āriyar ivarkaḷai, tāsyu, virātya eṉa aḻaittaṉar) anta ‘muntai āriyar’ tākkattiṉāl ak koḷkairikvēta āriyariṭamum paravi av vētattilum ēṟiyatu’. “cāvittiri toṉmamum ataṉuṭaiṉ toṭar puṭaiya caṭaṅkukaḷum cintu nākarika kālattilēyē uruvāṉavai.” 	        - askō parpōlā

The religion of pre-Vedic India goes back to a large extent to a Dravidian sub-stratum (linked with the Harappan culture) whose tradiitons were transmitted to Vedic times by an earlier wave of non-Vedic Aryans, the Dasa and Vratyas: Asko Parpola. 1981 Studia Orientalia, 50)

There is new evidence for the Harappan and Dravidian origin of many  conceptions and cultic practices that are central in later Indian religions, not only in  Saivism and Sakti - Tantrism, but in the Vedic ritual too. Asko Parpola (1985). The sky garment: A study of the Harappan religion and and its relation to the Mesopotamian and later Indian religions’ Studia Orientalia 57.

“Savitri and Resurrection: the ideal of devoted wife, her forehead mark, Sati and human sacrifice in Epic - Puranic, Vedic, Harappan - Dravidian, and Near - Eastern perspectives”: Asko Parpola; Studia Orientalia (1998). 

(vi) 1985 il veḷivanta South Asian Ardraeology 1983 nūlil ālciṉ karuttu “cintu nākarika mutunilaik kālattilēyē (2900 - 1500). intō - āriya moḻi pēcunar iṅku vantu cintu camayam - paṇpāṭṭuk kūṟukaḷai uḷvāṅkik koṇṭu piṉṉar rikvētam iṟuti vaṭivam peṟṟa ki.mu.1000ttil cintuc camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷ rik vētattil ēṟiyirukkavēṇṭum eṉpatākum. 

(vii) intu camayattil yāṇṭum kāṇappaṭum uruva vaḻipāṭu intiyāvil irunta āriyarallātār vaḻipāṭṭu muṟaikaḷil iruntē pirāmaṇīyattukku vantatu. (G.M. Bongard Levin: A complex study of Ancient India, 1986). 

(viii) mokañcotarō muttiraiyil kāṇappaṭupavar pōṉṟa (āriyarkaḷukku muntaiya intiya) yōkikaḷ vantēṟikaḷuṭaiya moḻiyaic cila kālattukkuḷ kaṟṟukkoṇṭu tamatu camaya maṟai poruṭkaḷai (vētap pāṭalkaḷai iyaṟṟiya) pirāmaṇarkaḷukkuk kaṟpikkat toṭaṅkiviṭṭaṉarō?” (vālparṭ: A New History of India; 1982)

(ix) āriya nāṭōṭikaḷiṉ muraṭṭup paḻakka vaḻakkaṅkaḷ, avarkaḷuṭaiya kaṭumaiyāṉa perumpōrkaḷ ākiyavaṟṟukkum ahimcaikkum (uyirkaḷukku ūṟu ceyyāmai), neṭuntolaivu ākaiyāl yōkam, tāyt teyva vaḻipāṭu pōṉṟavaṟṟaippōla ahimcaiyum āriyarkaḷukku muṟpaṭṭa (cintut tamiḻ) makkaḷiṭa miruntē avarkaḷ peṟṟatāka nām karutavēṇṭiyuḷḷatu. (vālparṭ: An Introduction to India; 1991)

(v) ār.ci. taṇṭā karuttuppaṭi piruku (caintavar), cukra, cyavaṉa, āṅkiras, mahitās, vaciṣṭar mutaliyavarkaḷ tirāviṭarē. intu matattil uḷḷa muṉivaḻipāṭu, peṇteyvavaḻipāṭu, pūcai, kōyil, uruva vaḻipāṭu, tantiram (Tantrism)  mutaliyavai tirāviṭak kūṟukaḷē; eṉṟum pala paḻaiya tirāviṭak kūṟukaḷ atarva vētattil uḷḷaṉa
eṉṟum avar karutukiṟār. mēlum avar vēta kāla āriyariṭam intiyāvil uruvākiya pirāmaṇa vakuppu pōṉṟu ēṉai nāṭukaḷil intō airōppiyariṭam uruvākavillai eṉpataiyum cuṭṭik kāṭṭukiṟār.

“The institution of Brahmins is unique to the Vedic Aryans. It has no parallels in pre-Christian Indo-European traditions. - R.C. Dhanda; p 166. 

14. iṉṟu ulakil uḷḷa camayaṅkaḷil ellām toṉmaiyāṉatu civa vaḻipāṭē eṉṟu mokañcotarō akaḻvāyvu  paṟṟiya 1931 āṇṭu nūlilēyē mārṣal cāṟṟiṉār. aṇmaikkāla varalāṟṟaṟiñar cilar karuttum ituvē: vālparṭ (1991) ‘civaṉ vaḻipāṭu vaṭa intiyāvaiviṭa teṉṉāṭṭiltāṉ makkaḷiṭam mutaṉmai peṟṟuḷḷatu teṉṉāṭṭut tirāviṭamakkaḷ āriyarkaḷukku neṭuṅkālattukku muṉṉarē aṅku nilaipeṟṟu iruntamaiyai itu kāṭṭuvatākalām. tāyt teyva vaḻipāṭum teṉṉāṭṭiltāṉ mikuntu kāṇappaṭukiṟatu.’

ji.em. rāparṭs:putiya peṅkuyiṉ ulaka varalāṟu (2007) ‘iṉṟu vaḻipaṭappaṭum intuk kaṭavuḷarkaḷil talaimaicciṟappu vāyntavar kaḷil oruvaṉ civaṉ. tolpaḻaṅkālattil iruntavaiyum makkaḷ iṉapperukkam, avarkaḷ celvapperukkam ākiyavaṟṟai kuṟik kōḷākak koṇṭavaiyumāṉa vaḻipāṭṭu muṟaikaḷ pala civaṉ vaḻi pāṭṭil iṇaintuḷḷaṉa. toṉmaiyāṉa civaṉ vaṭivam pōṉṟa oṉṟai mokañcōtarō muttirai oṉṟu koṇṭuḷḷatu. civaṉ aṭaiyāḷamākak koḷḷappaṭuvatum, taṟkālak kōyilkaḷil makkaḷ vaḻipaṭuvatumāṉa liṅkam pōṉṟa kaṟkaḷ hārappā nākarika eccaṅkaḷil uḷḷaṉa. eṉavē iṉṟu ulakattil niṉṟu nilavivarum camayaṅkaḷil civaṉ vaḻipāṭē aṉaittaiyumviṭat toṉmaivāyntatu eṉa uyttuṇarac cāṉṟukaḷ uḷḷaṉa.

15. tamiḻiyac (tirāviṭa) camayaṅkaḷ allāta ēṉaiyavaiyellām vilaṅkukaḷaik koṉṟu kaṭavuḷarkkup paṭaippataic ciṟanta vaḻi pāṭṭuc ceyalāka vitittatuṭaṉ maṉitaṉ uṇṇavum, kaṭavuḷarkkuk koṉṟu paṭaikkavumē vilaṅkukaḷai avaravar kaṭavuḷ paṭaittaṉar eṉat tamatu puṉita nūlkaḷil eḻuti vaittuḷḷa nilaiyil, tirāviṭar camayaṅkaḷāṉa camaṇamum pauttamumē kollā nōṉpai aṟivuṟuttiya māṇpuṭaiyavai eṉṟu kaṇṭōm. ac camayaṅkaḷiṉ vērkaḷ cintu veḷi nākarikattil iruntirukkavēṇṭum.

16. cāṅkiyam āriyarallātāruṭaiyatu; upaniṣattukkaḷ; cāṅkiyak karuttukaḷaik kaṭaṉ koṇṭuḷḷaṉa; eṉpatai mēlē kaṇṭōm. yōkamum āriyallātāriṭam (tirāviṭar - āstirik) uruvāṉa teṉpar ē.pi. karmarkar (Religions of India) ‘kuṭalai’ eṉṉum colliliruntu kuṇṭāliṉi’ uruvākiyatu eṉpar avar. viviyaṉ vorttiṅṭaṉum (A History of Yoga; Routledge and Kegan Paul; 1982) ikkaruttiṉarēyāvar:

1922l car jāṉ mārṣal mokoñcotarōvil akaḻnta poḻutu yōkam paṟṟiya mikap paḻaṅkālac cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭaittaṉa.

[pirāmaṇarkaḷiṉ vēta matattiṟku etirāṉavarkaḷum ataṟkup puṟampāṉavarkaḷum āṉa sramaṇa cuyacintaṉaivātikaḷ] vēta matattiṟku muṟpaṭṭa camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷilum aṟṟai nāḷ ulakil paravalāka iruntu vanta kōṭpāṭukaḷāṉa vilaṅkukaḷai (ēṉ maṉitarkaḷaiyum?) paliyiṭṭu kaṭavuḷarkaḷait tiruptippaṭuttutal, payirkaḷ nalla viḷaiccal taruvataṟkāṉa caṭaṅkukaḷ pōṉṟavaṟṟukkum etirāṉavarkaḷāka iruntirukkavēṇṭum. ittakaiya palavakaippaṭṭa camayac cuyacintaṉai iyakkaṅkaḷil yōkap payiṟci potuvāṉa kūṟāka iruntatu.

cāṅkiya tattuvamum vētakālattiṟku muntiyē intiyāvil tōṉṟiyatākat terikiṟatu.

17. cintunākarika makkaḷaip pūcāri aracarkaḷ Priest - kings āṇṭu vantaṉar eṉṟa mārṣal, vīlar ākiyōr karuttu ātāramaṟṟatu eṉpar pocel. (2002: 114-115) pūcāri - aracaṉ eṉa avarkaḷ cuṭṭum uruvam (DK 1909) pūcāriyumaṉṟu, aracaṉumaṉṟu, iraṇṭum kalantatu  malla; kaṟpaṉai uruvam eṉpār pocel.

18. piṟkāla intiya camūka - camaya amaippiṉ mutaṉmaik kūṟukaḷ palavum cintuveḷi nākarikattil kāṇappaṭṭaṉa eṉṟa vālparṭ (1991)ṉ kūṟṟu ivviyalukku muṭivuraiyāka amaiyum.

19. matam paṟṟik kūṟumpoḻutu iṉṉoṉṟaiyum iṟutiyākak karuttiṟ koḷvatu nalam: palvēṟu camayaṅkaḷ, avaṟṟiṉ meyyiyal pōṉṟavai paṟṟiya uṇarvellām ekkālattilum ennāṭṭilumpotu makkaḷil pattuviḻukkāṭṭukkum kuṟaivāṉavarkaḷiṭaiyētāṉ ōraḷavukku iruntirukkum. ēṉaiya 90 viḻukkāṭṭiṉar nilaivēṟu. ivvuṇmaiyai kaṉakacapaip piḷḷai (1904) aṉṟē kūṟiṉār: ‘ellā nāṭukaḷilum, ellāk kālaṅkaḷilum (paṇṭum, iṉṟum) uḷḷatupōl tamiḻariṭaiyēyum ittakaiya camayak kōṭpāṭukaḷukkum vekumakkaḷ naṭaimuṟaiyil kaṭaippiṭikkum camaya nampikkaikaḷ, caṭaṅkukaḷukkum perutta vēṟupāṭu uṇṭu (As usual among all nations, ancient and modern, the philosophic doctrines of the Tamils were far apart from the popular beliefs and ceremonies). ‘muntu tamiḻc’ cintu nākarika makkaḷ camayattukkum itu poruntuvatutāṉ. itē karuttai hec. ār. pēṭ tamatu tirunelvēli kecaṭṭiyaril (1917) piṉ varumāṟu kūṟiyuḷḷār:

ūrtōṟum potumakkaḷ maṉappūrvamāka ūkkattuṭaṉ vaḻipaṭum ērāḷamāṉa uḷḷūrt teyvaṅkaḷaip paṟṟi aṭuttu viḷakkappaṭukiṟatu. mikapperumpāṉmaiyāṉa makkaḷaip poruttavarait taṅkaḷ aṉṟāṭa vāḻkkai nikaḻccikaḷai nirṇayippavarkaḷum, (māntaṉ teyvamāka āṉāl eppaṭi naṭappāṉō appaṭi) ciṉam eṟpaṭṭāl kēṭuviḷaikkak kūṭiyavarkaḷum, pontikai ēṟpaṭṭāl kēṭu varāmal taṭuttum, vantāl nīkkiyum nalam ceyyak kūṭiyavarkaḷum āka avarkaḷ nampuvatu civaṉ, tirumālai alla: palvēṟu uruvaṅkaḷil vaḻipaṭappaṭum tāyt teyvamākiya kāḷi, maṟṟum uḷḷūrt teyvaṅkaḷāṉa cuṭalaimāṭaṉ maṟṟum atupōṉṟa ‘tuṭiyāṉa’ teyvaṅkaḷaiyē

“The accounts which will shortly be given of the hosts of lesser deities and of the enthusiasm with which they are worshipped, may serve to show that the gods who for the mass of the people rule and direct their daily lives, who bring evil and can if willing remove it, who are intelligible beings - men on the grand scale are not Siva and Vishnu but Kali in her various forms, and (local gods like) Sudalaimadan and his horrid crew.

iyal 7

cintu nākarikattukkum, elām, cumēriya,
ekiptu mutaliya nākarikaṅkaḷukkum uḷḷa
toṭarpupaṟṟiya hīrās (1953)
nūliṉ karuttukaḷ

potuvāka iṉṟu paṉṉāṭṭaṟiñar ēṟkum karuttu elām – cumēriya - ekiptu nākarikat tākkattāl tāṉ tamiḻiyac cintu nākarikamum uruvākiyirukkum; allatu cintu nākarikam uṭpaṭa aṉaittumē ēṟattāḻa camakālattil ciṟappaṭaintirukkum eṉpatutāṉ. cintu nākarikattiliruntu ēṉaiyavai tōṉṟiyirukkalāmeṉṉum karutukōḷai eṇṇip pārppataṟku icaivōrkūṭa oru cilarē; eṉiṉum akkarutukōḷai valuvāka muṉ vaittu 1953l Studies in Proto Indo Mediterraneam Culture  eṉṉum nuṇmāṇuḻaipulamikka āyvu nūlai (551 pakkam) veḷiyiṭṭavar heṉṟi hīrās pātiriyār, speyiṉ nāṭṭaic cērntavar. palvēṟu aṟiviyaṟ pulaṅkaḷil kaṭanta 60 āṇṭukaḷil nikaḻntuḷḷa vaḷarnilaiyiṉ aṭippaṭaiyilum kūṭa annūṟ ceytikaḷiṟ cila āḻntu karutat takkavaiyāy iruppatāl, avaṟṟiṉ curukkattai ivviyal tarukiṟatu. [ ] kuṟikaḷukkuḷ uḷḷavai maṭṭumē avar eḻutiyavai paṟṟiya cila karuttukaḷ.

(hīrās nūliṉ) aṟimukam 1 – 26

[intō āriya moḻi pēcunariṉ (ciṟueṇṇikkaiyil) nuḻaivukku muṉṉar] intiyāveṅkum tirāviṭamoḻi pēcunarē vacittu vantaṉar (heviṭ 1889; hāl 1913). rikvētam upaniṣattukkaḷ kālatti liruntē tirāviṭattākkam (viṉaikkoḷkai, maṟupiṟappu, yōkam) uḷḷatu. vēta, purāṇap pakutikaḷil pala paṇṭait tirāviṭa nūlkaḷiṉ moḻipeyarppukaḷē. [hīrācukku 22.11.1942 aṉṟu eḻutiya kaṭitattil vi.es. cuktaṅkar ‘yutiṣṭiraṉ katai āriyar varavukkum rikvēta ttukkum muntiyatu eṉpatu cariyāṉa karuttē’ eṉat terivittār.] nākarikamaṟṟa nilaiyil intiyāviṟkuḷ nuḻainta intō āriyar tamatu moḻiyai camskirutamāka (tiruntiya moḻiyāka) ākkikkoṇṭaṉar (They converted their rude matter - of - course speech -- a speech of shepherds and husbandmen - into a classical Sanskrit language)

tirāviṭamoḻi pēcunar naṇṇilakkarai nāṭukaḷiliruntu intiyāviṟkum teṉintiyāviṟkum vantavarkaḷ allar. iṅkiruntu aṅku ceṉṟu piṉṉar ulakeṅkum nākarikattaip parappiyavarkaḷ ivarkaḷē. ittuṟai āyvukaḷ niṟaivaṭaiyum poḻutu tirāviṭa nākarikamākiya cintunākarikam ulakanākarikattiṉ toṭṭil eṉpatu ēṟkappaṭum.

“the Dravidians of India, after a long period of development in this country, travelled westwards, and settling successively in the various lands, they found their way from Mesopotamia upto the British isles, spread their race – afterwards named Mediterranean owing to the place where they were known anthropologically -- through the west and made their civilization flourish in two continents, being thus the originators of the modern world civilization. The Mediterranean nations of the ancientworld were racial off-shoots of the mighty proto-Indian race”

[After the problems of decipherment of Indus script and those of the migration of Dravidian civilisation out of India to Elam, Sumeria and the west are solved.] India will be acknowledged as the cradle of human civilization.

iyal I: mokeñcotarō eḻuttai vācittal 29-158.

cintu eḻuttiliruntu tāṉ pirami eḻuttu tōṉṟiyatu. atu karutteḻuttu ideo-phonographic; lipiyāṉatu Picto – Phonographic . avai acaiyeḻuttu alla; meyyeḻuttu (maṭṭum) alla; muḻumaiyāṉa coṟkaḷaik kuṟippavai; vivaraṇaikaḷ, kūṟṟukaḷ mutaliyavai ākum. cintu eḻuttuppoṟippukaḷaip piṉvaruvaṉavaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭaiyil paṭippatutāṉ poruttamākum:

(1) cintu nākarikam āriyaruṭaiyatu aṉṟu; tirāviṭar uṭaiyatu; (tirāviṭar naṇṇilakkarai nākarikaṅkaḷiṉ makkaḷuṭaṉ toṭar puṭaiyavarkaḷ. eṉavē cintup poṟippukaḷukkum cumēriyam, ekiptu, etruskaṉ, miṉōvaṉ mutaliya poṟippukaḷukkum cila oppumaikaḷ irukkum); cintunākarika makkaḷ pēciyatu toltirāviṭa moḻi.

(2) (cintu nākarikat) tol tirāviṭamoḻiyiṉ ilakkaṇam vaḷarcciyaṭaiyāta nilaiyil iruntirukkum.

(3) (i) muttiraikaḷaip patittup paṭikkum vācakaṅkaḷai valamiruntu iṭamāka L<--- R  paṭikkavēṇṭum. Symbol perumpālum iṭatu kōṭiyil īṟṟu vaṭivamāka varuvatu ‘atu’ (māṟaṉatu) eṉpatiṟ pōl uṭaimaip poruḷil, allatu cilavaṭivaṅkaḷil – valakkōṭiyil vantāl (cuṭṭup poruḷil).
